# TODO — Open Point List

HOW TO USE:

- *Features* are represented as 2nd level Headings.
- New *Features* shall normally be added to the top of list
- Features have an ID. 
- They consist of *Tasks* whose prefix corresponds to the Feature's ID.
- A *Feature* is implementation-complete when its declared Tasks/Subtasks have an implementation/disposition result. It is ready to close when, in addition, every integration checkpoint the architect declared within it has a current passing privileged integration review and every required transitive `[x]`/`[w]` predecessor in each resulting Task-Acceptance batch has its own current `Acceptance: ✓`, bottom-up — including the Feature aggregate review when the Feature node itself is flagged. An unflagged Task does not independently trigger an integration review, and missing Acceptance does not block ordinary successor implementation; if that Task is an unaccepted required predecessor of a Task now being accepted, however, it enters the same Acceptance batch and receives its own decision. See **Integration checkpoints and the architect** below and `DEC-0044-020`.
- A Feature may be moved to `DONE.md` only by a privileged agent, after every required integration checkpoint passes and all Feature prerequisites pass. `TODO.md` and `DONE.md` must be committed after each accepted Feature move. Historical Features already in `DONE.md` retain their recorded pre-policy evidence status and are not retroactively represented as accepted.

- *Tasks* are dashed items whose first line carries an implementation marker and task ID. Supporting acceptance-criteria, Definition-of-Done, decision, review, acceptance, and history bullets may follow.
  [ ] - open. No work has been done w/r to this item.
  [u] - unclear. No agentic work can currently be performed on this item because user/manager discussion, authorization, or clarification is required before proceeding.
  [p] - partially implemented. The agent has started work on this item, but it is not yet complete; use this while work is in progress, including across conversations, so agents can determine the next best unfinished item.
  [?] - unknown. The next step is to investigate the repository and decide whether to amend the Task or begin implementation.
  [d] - deferred. No human decision is required. An agent has determined that work was performed on this item but cannot currently continue because prerequisites for the work are not met. On completion of a predecessor — that is, when the predecessor moves to `[x]` — the worker re-examines this item's prerequisites and then sets it to `[ ]`, `[x]`, `[p]`, or `[u]` accordingly. Without locking two agents may re-examine the same `[d]` item concurrently; that is tolerated, because a `[d]` item can be revisited at any time. Distinguish from `[u]`: `[u]` means a human decision is the next action, `[d]` means it is not.
  [w] - disposition-complete, awaiting acceptance. Investigation supports closing without implementation because the issue is invalid, non-reproducible, superseded, duplicate, cancelled, or otherwise not worth implementing. Record a `Reason:` and real disposition `REF`.
  [x] - executed, awaiting acceptance. The deliverable, required implementer validation, findings disposition, and real substantive `REF` are committed.
- Task acceptance is an orthogonal state rendered by an exact `**Acceptance:** ✓` record beneath the `[x]`/`[w]` Task. The record retains the accepted disposition, authorized reviewer identity/assignment, ISO-8601 time, exact contract/work-product/prerequisite digests, and full reachable review `REF` as defined by `docs/pipeline/task-acceptance.md`. Only an explicitly assigned, currently privileged, normally independent reviewer may create, change, invalidate, or remove current acceptance credit. Sandboxed/grunt agents are prohibited from doing so.
- A Task's imperative sentence and any **Acceptance criteria** bullets are normative. The sentence must identify an observable deliverable or result; add explicit acceptance criteria and a **Definition of Done** whenever completion, failure behavior, evidence, or review authority would otherwise be ambiguous.
- Unless a Task states a stricter gate, `[x]` requires the committed deliverable, specified validation evidence, resolution or explicit disposition of material findings, and the commit `REF`; `[w]` requires the committed disposition evidence, reason, and `REF`. Neither state alone permits Feature closure.

- Tasks shall have a granularity so that they can be implemented in one go. If preparation or implementation is agentic but a product/risk/release decision requires a named human authority, split the work or keep it `[p]`; set `[u]` only when that human decision is the next unresolved action. Task work-product acceptance remains a separately assigned privileged review and must not be confused with those specialist decisions.
- A Task/Subtask prerequisite is ordinarily an **implementation start gate**: `[x]`/`[w]` satisfies it so work can continue without serial privileged review. A Task may explicitly require a predecessor's current acceptance before start when it consumes a high-risk canonical contract, irreversible migration, credential/security boundary, release, or comparable decision. **Task Acceptance is a separate, prerequisite-closed gate:** when Acceptance is assigned for a target, every required transitive `[x]`/`[w]` predecessor without current valid Acceptance enters the same review batch, receives its own decision bottom-up, and must be accepted before the target can receive current `✓` credit. A current valid Acceptance boundary stops this expansion; an unmarked predecessor is not exempt. See `DEC-0044-020`.
- A Feature prerequisite is a **Feature-closure gate**: individual Tasks may proceed when their own start gates permit, but the dependent Feature cannot move to `DONE.md` first. Conditional or alternative lifecycle dependencies must be materialized after the governing scope decision as explicit prerequisites or machine-enforced selected-profile edges; prose-only dependencies are insufficient.
- Agents shall keep implementation markers and acceptance history accurate. Set `[p]` once implementation/investigation starts, use `[u]` only as defined above, finalize the implementation claim at `[x]`/`[w]`, and preserve rejected, inconclusive, superseded, or invalidated acceptance attempts append-only.

<!-- BEGIN adversarial-completion-evidence@v1 -->
**Adversarial completion evidence (`DEC-0038-004`).** This requirement is **claim-bound**: it is triggered by the behavior or contract a substantive change alters, never by file type, by whether an implementer or a reviewer executes the case, or by whether the existing suite is green.

- **AE-1 — Applicability.** The requirement applies when a substantive change alters **counting or cardinality**, **identity matching**, **serialization shape or field presence**, or **blocking/gate classification**, or when it **asserts an invariant over a set or sequence**. Merely touching a file associated with those topics does not trigger it; presenting such a behavior change as documentation-only or test-only work does not avoid it.
- **AE-2 — Exact baselines.** The evidence identifies the exact **pre-change baseline** and the exact **candidate** against which it was produced.
- **AE-3 — Falsification case, red on the baseline.** The evidence names at least one falsification case **derived from the changed contract**, and shows it **red on the pre-change behavior and green on the candidate**, with the real command and bounded output or an immutable output reference. Red-first binds those two baselines, not the wall-clock order in which the case was authored. A case that was always green, a mocked assertion that bypasses the changed path, and a prose claim are each nonconforming.
- **AE-4 — At least two named adjacent cases.** The evidence names at least **two distinct adjacent contract cases**, each with its **neighboring dimension, expected result, observed result, and why it is adjacent**. A case that turned out fine is conforming; naming none is not. The falsification case may count as one of the two only when it exercises a genuinely distinct neighbor rather than restating the happy path.
- **AE-5 — Property evidence for set and sequence claims.** When the change claims an invariant over a set or sequence — deduplication, union or merge, closure, membership, multiplicity, ordering — the evidence additionally includes a **generative or exhaustive property test**, naming its **invariant or oracle**, its **generation domain or finite enumeration boundary**, its **seed or replay input** where applicable, and its **actual executed case count**. No universal minimum count is imposed; the reviewer judges whether the domain meaningfully exercises the claim.
- **AE-6 — Additive only.** These requirements **add to** existing unit, integration, safety, regression, checkpoint, Acceptance, provenance, independence, and authority evidence. They replace and weaken nothing, and they never convert an authority question into a test-only question.
- **AE-7 — Explicit exclusions.** Prose-only and documentation-only changes, bookkeeping and marker/claim maintenance, unrelated generated-output refreshes, and authority, baseline, or independence questions are **out of scope**. An excluded change remains subject to every pre-existing requirement.
- **AE-8 — Both projections or neither.** This block is normative only while it appears with identical meaning in **both** the `TODO.md` header contract and the completion section of `AGENTS.md`. A partial or divergent projection is nonconforming and must not be treated as active policy.
<!-- END adversarial-completion-evidence@v1 -->

## ID scheme

- *Feature names* are kept consistent in English (translate on introduction if needed).

- Every *Feature* gets a unique **feature ID**: a 4-digit number with leading zeroes, e.g. `0001`. Feature headings are written as `## Feature: XXXX — <name>`.

- Every *Task* within a feature gets a **task ID** `XXXX-YY`, where `XXXX` is the feature ID and `YY` is a 2-digit task number, unique within that feature (e.g. `0001-01`, `0001-02`). Task IDs are rendered in bold right after the checkbox marker, e.g. `- [ ] **0001-01** ...`.

- A *Task* may be split into **subtasks**, identified as `XXXX-YY.ZZ`, where `ZZ` is a 2-digit subtask number unique within that task (e.g. `0001-01.01`, `0001-01.02`).

- *Tasks* and *Features* may declare **prerequisites** — other tasks/features that must be done first. One prerequisite relation is written as:

  `XXXX[-YY[.ZZ]]:AAAA[-BB[.CC]]`

  - `XXXX` — feature ID of the dependent item
  - `-YY` / `.ZZ` — optional dependent task/subtask; omitted means the whole feature depends on the prerequisite
  - `AAAA` — feature ID of the prerequisite
  - `-BB` / `.CC` — optional prerequisite task/subtask; omitted means the dependency is on the whole feature
  - Multiple prerequisites are a comma-separated list of complete relations; the dependent ID is repeated in each relation.

  Examples:
  - `0002:0001` — Feature `0002` as a whole depends on Feature `0001` as a whole.
  - `0002-09:0001` — Task `0002-09` depends on Feature `0001` as a whole.
  - `0002-09:0001-08` — Task `0002-09` depends specifically on Task `0001-08`.
  - `0006-04.02:0006-04.01` — Subtask `0006-04.02` depends on sibling Subtask `0006-04.01`.
  - `0014-13:0015-06, 0014-13:0016-01` — Task `0014-13` has two prerequisites.

  Prerequisites are noted inline in the task/feature text after `PREREQ:`.

## Branch model and integration

- Feature/Task/Subtask work is carried on Git branches named after the item ID (`0038`, `0038-01`, `0038-01.01`) and merged upward; the full procedure is [`docs/pipeline/branch-workflow.md`](docs/pipeline/branch-workflow.md).
- Claim files are committed on the item's branch alongside work products and travel with every merge; they are not deleted at `[x]`/`[w]` but reconciled and removed by the privileged integrator at Feature integration.
- A worker bases its branch off the parent (Task off the Feature branch, Subtask off the Task branch) and, as its first mutating step, merges in every done-but-unintegrated (`[x]`/`[w]`) prerequisite branch so predecessor work products and claim files are present.
- **Subtask→Task** merges may be performed by a sandboxed/grunt agent; **Task→Feature** integration and **Feature→`main`** closure are privileged-only.
- When a privileged integrator cannot approve the work at an integration checkpoint, it records a `[u]` **integration verdict** beneath that node and hands resolution to an explicit user interaction; this reuses the `[u]` meaning at integration granularity.

## Integration checkpoints and the architect

- **Integration review is attribute-driven, per node.** Only a Task, Subtask, or Feature carrying `**Integration review:** mandatory` independently triggers an integration review. This does **not** exclude unmarked required predecessors from the prerequisite-closed Task-Acceptance batch induced by that review: every such `[x]`/`[w]` predecessor without current valid Acceptance receives its own bottom-up decision before the marked target can receive current `Acceptance: ✓`. Nor is checkpoint placement frozen at decomposition: a management-instantiated architect may add the attribute, with recorded rationale, at any time before the affected node has current Acceptance, including while it is `[x]`/`[w]`. See `DEC-0044-020`.
- **The architect sets it.** The *architect* is an authority **instantiated by management**, whose job is to subdivide a Feature into bounded, context-rich work packages so implementers need minimal reasoning, and to review the resulting tasks and flag the most critical ones `Integration review: mandatory`, with recorded rationale. Sandboxed/grunt implementers never set, clear, or move the attribute. Current Acceptance closes the Architect's ordinary designation window for that accepted baseline; a later addition, removal, or movement requires separately authorized append-only invalidation or reopening first and never rewrites history. Immediately before Acceptance bookkeeping, the reviewer must compare-and-swap the expected Task block, checkpoint attribute, contract digest, prerequisite graph, and Acceptance state; intervening Architect change makes the pinned review stale. The architect is distinct from the implementer and from the integrator who performs the review (separation of *who scopes*, *who builds*, *who reviews*).
- **Cross-item gate scope is decided and reviewed before mutation.** During Feature breakdown and again before implementation, apply the canonical `cross-item-blast-radius` predicate from [`decision-record@v1`](docs/pipeline/decision-record.md#2-wann-ein-datensatz-verpflichtend-ist): qualifying declared gate behavior can block the start, validation, acceptance, integration, publication, or closure of another work unit, or change that other unit's contract. A shared path, difficulty, unfamiliarity, green validation, or a hypothetical ordinary bug is not sufficient.
- **The pre-mutation gate is narrow and mandatory.** Before the first mutation that implements, activates, widens, narrows, affirmatively retains, or removes a qualifying gate scope, a conforming `decision-record@v1` and a supporting scope review by a management-instantiated architect whose identity is distinct from the Implementer's identity must exist. Affirmative retention is a deliberate in-scope decision to preserve existing, already-contested gate behavior; passive inheritance is excluded. The review is a scope-authority check, not Task acceptance, integration review, an integration verdict, or `Acceptance: ✓`; green validation does not establish scope correctness, completeness, or authority.
- **Preparation remains agentic.** Keep the item `[p]` while affected-unit/gate analysis, decision-record preparation, or assigned-architect review can proceed. Use `[u]` only when architect assignment, an authority decision, dissent resolution, or a management exception is the sole next action.
- **Every Feature breakdown includes an integrating task.** The feature-breakdown process requires the architect to create exactly one **integration task** that integrates the whole Feature's work, and to create it with `Integration review: mandatory` set. This is the Feature's terminal integration checkpoint and its effective review floor — declared explicitly in the backlog rather than implied by hierarchy. The architect may add further intermediate checkpoints for large or risky Features at decomposition or later, but only before current Acceptance of the affected node and subject to the decision-record/scope-review gate above.
- **Management may change or circumvent the process.** Real work produces surprises, and management — the current user or a registered authority above the process — retains the power to waive the mandatory integrating task, override an integrator's `[u]` verdict, or authorize closure without a required review. Such an override is valid only as an explicit, recorded act naming the authority, scope, reason, and any compensating controls; it is never taken autonomously by an architect, integrator, implementer, or grunt, and it deletes no history (the waived requirement and the override both remain visible).
- **Rationale is mandatory in both directions.** Setting the attribute records *why* the node is a checkpoint. A node that touches an irreversible migration, external effect, credential/security boundary, or public release and is left **unflagged** must record an explicit **no-checkpoint justification**, so an omission is an auditable decision rather than a silent gap.
- **A checkpoint gates upward integration, not implementation start.** Dependents may still implement on a checkpoint's `[x]`/`[w]` and merge it in; only crossing the checkpoint boundary upward — and treating its work as *integrated* — waits for the integrator's passing review.
- **Authority follows the attribute, not the level.** Merging a checkpoint node's work across its boundary requires the privileged integrator; merges that cross no checkpoint stay grunt-eligible. The attribute is orthogonal to the `[ ]/[p]/[x]/[w]` marker and to `Acceptance: ✓`: the attribute is the *requirement* that a review occur, and `Acceptance: ✓` is its *fulfillment*.
- A Feature moves to `DONE.md` once its work is terminal and every integration checkpoint within it — including the Feature node itself, if flagged — has a current passing integration review; the `DONE.md` move is always a privileged act. See [`docs/pipeline/branch-workflow.md`](docs/pipeline/branch-workflow.md) and [`docs/pipeline/task-acceptance.md`](docs/pipeline/task-acceptance.md).

## Feature: 0044 — Process Improvement: Integration Policy, Architecture Process, and Capability-Based Task Matching

**Authority:** Customer brainstorming essay of 2026-08-20, recorded verbatim as `RQ-SRC-04` with decisions `DEC-0044-001..004` in [`docs/dossiers/re-intake-prozessverbesserung-integration-und-capabilities.md`](docs/dossiers/re-intake-prozessverbesserung-integration-und-capabilities.md).

**Goal:** Make the architecture, RE, and integration processes traceable and mechanically checkable: a binding integration-policy semantics (target-branch policy governs; planning errors prevented; risk integration gated by unanimity or user decision), a breakdown process instruction that states *what architecture decisions, task dependencies, and test cases are derived from*, and a machine-readable capability model so the orchestrator — not the architect — selects the right agent per task without needing AI for the matching itself.

**Effective immediately (already anchored at intake):** Per `DEC-0044-001..003`, the target-branch policy governs every integration, policy changes may be pulled in from the integration target, no agent commits policy changes that originated on a foreign branch, and risk integrations require Integrator+QA+Architect unanimity or user escalation. See the "Integration policy precedence" section of [`docs/pipeline/branch-workflow.md`](docs/pipeline/branch-workflow.md); Tasks `0044-01`/`0044-02` complete the elaboration and machine checks.

**Role assignment (customer directive):** RE and architecture elaboration belongs to the specialist personas of [`docs/pipeline/process-roles.md`](docs/pipeline/process-roles.md); the tasks below marked *architect-elaboration* are to be claimed by sessions acting in that role, not by generalist implementers.

**Feature Definition of Done:** The policy-precedence semantics, planning-error prevention, and risk-integration procedure are anchored in the authority documents with at least one mechanical check; the breakdown process instruction exists and names its derivation sources; task requirement profiles and agent capability descriptors have machine-readable schemas plus a deterministic matcher; the cognitive-demand estimation question has a documented, calibratable answer; and the role catalog has been reviewed against the new model with recorded outcomes.

- [x] **0044-01** Anchor the full integration-policy semantics in the process model and make policy provenance mechanically checkable. Claim: `TODO-seven-rebi-0044-01-20260820T235543Z.md`. REF: `decfce912` (read-only provenance tool + tests + tools.md registration, Implementer seven-rebi), `ba024524b` (Architect seven-bellana gate-scope review, DEC-0044-005/006, branch-workflow.md A1–A4/`0044-04` prose completion), `711095a5b` (first corrective fix, since found incomplete by the second `[u]` verdict below), superseded/corrected by `36d048ce2` (Implementer seven-rebi, second corrective fix — classification rewritten to use commit topology instead of branch containment entirely — see "Second corrective implementation" beneath the second verdict), followed by `fc466afb2` (Implementer Seven-Kael, third corrective implementation — docstring-only fast-forward blind spot documentation per `DEC-0044-007`, no classification-logic change — see "Third corrective implementation" beneath the Architect disposition note under the third verdict). Original re-verification note (2026-08-20, since found incomplete — kept for history, not deleted): 6/6 `test_check_policy_provenance.py` tests pass; tool runs clean read-only against real branches (`0044-01` vs `main`, including classifying `ba024524b` itself as `source-origin`); `branch-workflow.md` states precedence, explicit A1–A4, replacement rule + `TK-2` duty (`DEC-0044-005`), foreign-branch prohibition, and pull-in permission consistently with `AGENTS.md`/`task-acceptance.md`; `0044-04` confirmed to exist as the referenced A1/A2 prevention point; no `DEC-CAP-001`/`DEC-0041-002` contradiction found.     - **Verdict author:** `agent:seven-tom:integrator:0044-01-review:20260821T000000Z` (privileged Integrator persona, independent of claim owner `seven-rebi` and Architect `seven-b'ellana`).

    - **Reason:** Independently reran `_src/tools/test_check_policy_provenance.py` (6/6 pass — unit tests are not the problem) and then exercised the tool for real, exactly as this review's briefing and the tool's own intended usage require: from an isolated worktree checked out at the `0044-01` branch tip (`git worktree add ... refs/heads/0044-01`), comparing `0044-01` against `main`. Expected/claimed result (per this Task's own `[x]` line and the Implementer's closure note): `ba024524b` classified `source-origin`. Actual, reproduced result:
      ```
      $ python3 _src/tools/check_policy_provenance.py --source-branch 0044-01 --target-branch main --json
      exit 1
      "classification": "foreign-branch"
      "containing_branches": ["(no branch)", "0044-01"]
      "note": "... is also reachable from a branch other than source/target: (no branch). Flag for review under DEC-0044-002 ..."
      ```
      Root cause (read in `_src/tools/check_policy_provenance.py:_branches_containing`, which shells out to `git branch --all --contains <sha> --format=%(refname:short)`): a detached-HEAD worktree at or below the inspected commit prints a synthetic `(no branch)` line, which the tool treats as an unrecognized third branch and therefore as a foreign-branch policy commit. The exact same false positive reproduces from a second, non-detached local branch checked out at the same tip (confirmed by checking out `0044-01` under a second local branch name and rerunning both directions) — the classifier's "other branches containing this commit" check does not distinguish a genuine foreign lineage from (a) the reviewer's own detached-HEAD worktree, or (b) any other local ref/branch that happens to also contain the commit (e.g. a later Task branch built on top, or a second worktree of the same branch) — both of which are routine, policy-sanctioned states in this repository (`AGENTS.md`: branches and their claim files are retained, not deleted, after merge; this session's own briefing instructs creating an isolated worktree via exactly the pattern that triggers the bug). The tool's core claimed behavior — correctly telling a real foreign-branch policy commit apart from a benign one — is therefore not reliable under the tool's own intended and instructed usage; the Implementer's re-verification note asserting a clean `source-origin` classification for `ba024524b` does not reproduce.
    - **Integration-branch tip:** `9716738fa0cae1903f0adf35373576888cc425c3` (unchanged `0044-01` tip; nothing further was merged). This verdict itself is recorded on a separate, non-integrating review branch, `0044-01-integration-review-seven-tom-20260821T000000Z`, based on `0044-01` at that same tip.
    - **Disposition:** Not merged into `0044`; no `Acceptance: ✓` created. Corrective work (fix `_branches_containing`/classification to exclude the reviewer's own detached-HEAD/worktree artifacts and any branch relationship that is not evidence of foreign origin, e.g. ancestor/descendant of source, then re-verify against real branches from a genuinely independent worktree) is actionable implementation work; recommend returning `0044-01` to `[p]` for that repair under a resumed or new claim, followed by a fresh independent review. All other elements of this Task (prose in `branch-workflow.md`, `DEC-0044-005`/`DEC-0044-006`, `0044-04` cross-reference, `tools.md` registration, `AGENTS.md`/`task-acceptance.md`/`DEC-CAP-001`/`DEC-0041-002` consistency) were independently re-verified in this review and found sound; only the mechanical-check tool's core classification is in question.
    - **Corrective implementation (seven-rebi, resumed claim, `TODO-seven-rebi-0044-01-20260820T235543Z.md`):** This `[u]` verdict is retained verbatim above as the historical review record — the Implementer does not clear another agent's verdict; only the verdict author or the user does. Fixed both false-positive causes Tom identified/implied in `_src/tools/check_policy_provenance.py`: (1) `_branches_containing()` now filters the synthetic `(no branch)` line `git branch --all --contains` emits for a detached-HEAD checkout (own or another worktree of this repo), which is never a real branch; (2) the foreign-branch classifier now also excludes any real named branch whose tip is `source_commit` itself or a descendant of it (e.g. Tom's own `0044-01-integration-review-seven-tom-20260821T000000Z` review branch, or any later Task branch chained on top of `0044-01`) — such a branch trivially contains every source commit through plain ancestry, not because the commit originated there; a branch whose tip is instead an *ancestor* of `source_commit` remains correctly flagged (the genuine `DEC-0044-002` shape). Added two regression tests reproducing Tom's exact scenarios: a detached-HEAD worktree at the source tip queried from both the primary and the detached worktree, and a downstream review/Task branch chained onto source — `_src/tools/test_check_policy_provenance.py` now has 8/8 passing. Re-verified live against the real repository from a second isolated detached-HEAD worktree at `ba024524b` (Tom's exact reproduction shape): `check_policy_provenance.py --source-branch 0044-01 --target-branch main --json` now returns `exit 0`, `ba024524b` classified `source-origin`, `has_foreign_branch_policy_commit: false` — matching the originally claimed (and now actually reproduced) result, from both the primary worktree and the detached one. **This claim intentionally does not self-clear the `[u]` verdict or add `Acceptance: ✓`** — per the coordinator's routing, a fresh independent integration review is required before this branch is merged anywhere further.

The detached-HEAD `(no branch)` false positive and the downstream-review-branch false positive are both genuinely fixed. `branch-workflow.md` and `docs/pipeline/tools.md` are byte-for-byte unchanged since the last review (`git diff 9716738fa 3a6e0c5e0 --stat` touches only `TODO.md`, the claim file, and `_src/tools/check_policy_provenance{,_test}.py`) — no regression there; decision records and `0044-04` cross-reference likewise unaffected.
      - **New defect found (third false-positive shape):** The `_is_ancestor()` fix only excludes a branch whose tip is `source_commit` itself or *downstream* of it. It does not exclude a branch whose tip is *upstream* of `source_commit` but still on `source`'s own direct/first-parent line (e.g. an earlier checkpoint, stale WIP branch, or old review branch pointing at an earlier commit of `0044-01` itself) — that branch is still misclassified as foreign, even though it never diverged from `source`'s own history at all. Minimal reproduction, from a fresh worktree, with no dependency on any previously-created branch:
        ```
        $ git branch stale-wip-branch 9716738fa   # an ancestor commit on 0044-01's own line
        $ python3 _src/tools/check_policy_provenance.py --source-branch 0044-01 --target-branch main --json
        exit 1
        "classification": "foreign-branch"
        "containing_branches": ["0044-01", "0044-01-integration-review-seven-tom-20260821T000000Z", "stale-wip-branch"]
        ```
        (`stale-wip-branch` was deleted again immediately after reproducing this; it was created only in the isolated review worktree for the test.) Root cause: `check_policy_provenance()` (around the `others.append(b)` loop) only skips `b` when `branch_tip == source_commit or _is_ancestor(source_commit, branch_tip)` (branch at-or-downstream of source). It never checks the converse — `_is_ancestor(branch_tip, source_commit)` combined with `branch_tip` lying on `source`'s own direct ancestry (as opposed to a genuinely separate lineage later joined by a merge commit) — so any stale local branch/tag pointing at an earlier point in `source`'s own linear history still triggers a false `foreign-branch` flag for every policy commit after that point. This is exactly as routine an artifact as the previous two (old review/WIP branches are never deleted in this repository) and defeats the same acceptance criterion again.

Re-ran all three previously reported false-positive shapes together from a fresh worktree — the original detached-HEAD `(no branch)` case, a downstream review branch, and a stale WIP branch pointing at an ancestor commit (`git branch stale-wip-branch 9716738fa`) — all three now correctly agree: `ba024524b` classified `source-origin`, `exit 0`, `has_foreign_branch_policy_commit: false`. `git diff 3a6e0c5e0 bc01f6a76 --stat` touches only `TODO.md`, the claim file, and the two `check_policy_provenance{,_test}.py` files — `branch-workflow.md`/`tools.md` unchanged, no regression.
      - **New defect found (false negative, not another false positive):** The redesign bases `source-origin` purely on first-parent-chain membership plus non-merge-commit status. Git has no way to distinguish, from topology alone, "authored directly on `source`" from "an unrelated, genuinely foreign commit that reached `source`'s tip via a **fast-forward** (`git merge --ff-only`/`git update-ref`, i.e. no merge commit is ever created)" — a fast-forwarded foreign commit sits on the receiving branch's first-parent chain and has exactly one parent, identical in shape to a commit truly authored there. This is not a hypothetical: fast-forward-via-`update-ref` is the exact mechanism this session's own integration workflow uses to advance `main` (per this review's own instructions), so an equivalent operation on a Task branch — pulling in a newer commit from another branch via `git merge --ff-only` rather than a real merge — is a routine, unremarkable-looking git operation, not misuse. Minimal reproduction (fresh, isolated scratch repo, independent of `0044-01`):
        ```
        # target-branch at T0; foreign-branch diverges from T0, adds a policy-path commit
        # source-branch is created at T0 (has NOT diverged from target yet), then does
        # `git merge --ff-only foreign-branch` — a pure fast-forward, zero merge commits
        $ python3 check_policy_provenance.py --source-branch source-branch --target-branch target-branch --json
        exit 0
        "classification": "source-origin"
        "containing_branches": ["foreign-branch", "source-branch"]
        "has_foreign_branch_policy_commit": false
        ```
        `containing_branches` correctly lists `foreign-branch` — the evidence is present in the informational field — but the redesign explicitly never uses that field for classification (by design, to fix the three prior false positives), so the tool reports the merge candidate clean. This is a **false negative** against the Task's own Acceptance Criterion ("flag a foreign-branch policy commit") and against `DEC-0044-002` itself: it is the actual violation shape the check exists to catch, silently missed, which is a materially worse failure mode than the three prior false alarms (a false positive merely costs a manual look; this false negative would let a genuine foreign-branch policy change through as clean). Not covered by the module docstring's "residual known limitations" section, which lists history rewrites and identical-tree cherry-picks but not fast-forward absorption.

This third verdict is recorded on a separate, non-integrating review branch, `0044-01-integration-review-seven-tom-20260821T020000Z`, based on `0044-01` at that same tip.
      - **Disposition:** Still not merged into `0044`; still no `Acceptance: ✓`. This is a structural limitation of pure-topology classification, not a one-line patch: git literally does not record which branch a fast-forwarded commit was "really" authored on, so no purely-local-history signal can close this gap with certainty. Options for a next pass include: (a) explicitly documenting this as an accepted residual limitation (added to the docstring's limitations section) with a recommended companion process control — e.g. prohibiting `--ff-only`/fast-forward absorption of a *different* branch's tip onto a Task branch that is not itself that branch's designated successor, or requiring an explicit merge commit whenever pulling in commits from any branch other than the direct predecessor chain — since a purely mechanical check cannot fully replace that discipline; or (b) a weaker heuristic using reflogs / recorded branch-creation timestamps where available, understood to be best-effort, not authoritative. Either way this needs a recorded decision (`TK-2`, reach beyond this Task) and re-verification, not a silent tool patch. Followed, as before, by a fresh independent review from a different, previously-unused worktree before this branch moves further.

Im Wortlaut: „Ja, es soll diese Befugnis geben, ja. Wenn drei privilegierte Agenten einstimmig entscheiden, dann darf eine Regel ausser Kraft gesetzt werden, wobei QA-Manager und Security-Manager jeweils ein Veto-Recht besitzen." Damit ist der Entwurf von `Data-Nora` in **zwei** Punkten geaendert: die entscheidende Instanz sind **drei privilegierte Agenten** (nicht die urspruenglich vorgeschlagene feste Besetzung Integrator/QA/Architekt), und es gibt ein **eigenes Veto-Recht fuer QA-Manager und Security-Manager**. Der **Security-Manager** kam im Entwurf ueberhaupt nicht vor. **Offener Punkt, der vor dem Datensatz zu klaeren ist:** ob QA-Manager und Security-Manager zu den drei Entscheidenden zaehlen (dann waere das Veto in der Einstimmigkeit bereits enthalten und bezoege sich vermutlich auf ein **nachtraegliches** Veto) oder ob sie ausserhalb des Dreiergremiums ein unabhaengiges Veto halten. Das ist keine Formulierungsfrage — es bestimmt, wer ueberhaupt gefragt werden muss. Kein Agent entscheidet das durch Auslegung. **ZUSAMMENSETZUNG RATIFIZIERT (2026-08-22, Management):** Im Wortlaut: „koennen im Gremium sitzen, vetorecht gilt aber auch von aussen." Damit ist der harte Halt aus `DEC-0044-018` aufgehoben. QA-Manager und Security-Manager **duerfen** Mitglied des einstimmigen Dreiergremiums sein, muessen es aber nicht; **ihr Veto besteht unabhaengig davon und gilt auch von aussen**. Folge: beide sind **immer zu befassen** — eine Aussetzung, die an einem von beiden vorbeigeht, ist ungueltig. Sitzt keiner im Gremium, ist die Einstimmigkeit **notwendig, aber nicht hinreichend**; Schweigen ist keine Zustimmung, die Befassung ist nachzuweisen. Ein Veto ist fuer den jeweiligen Antrag **endgueltig** und wird auch durch Einstimmigkeit nicht ueberstimmt. Vollstaendig in `docs/dossiers/dec-branching-merging-strategie.md`. All worktrees share the common `.git` directory, so one hook covers every worktree.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** A stdlib-only hook detects and logs foreign-origin absorption at transaction time; it never blocks a legitimate operation of the documented integration pattern (fast-forward to an item's own already-integrated successor tip); an installation path and a check that the hook is present exist; the hook's own failure never corrupts a ref transaction; tests cover ff-merge, `update-ref`, and the legitimate carve-outs.

This must be stated where the hook is documented.
  - **Integration review: mandatory.** **Rationale (provisional — conservative default, not an architect decision):** a control agents may come to rely on, and over-trusting it is precisely the failure mode `DEC-0044-009` warns about. Weighing against a checkpoint: the hook changes no authority and blocks nothing. Set by Projektleiter Kathryn as the conservative default because only an architect may record a no-checkpoint justification; to be confirmed or downgraded by the architect, at the latest at `0044-08`.
    - **Architect checkpoint decision (2026-08-24, Architect `seven`, Seven of Nine; recorded per `process-roles.md` §Architect and the `AGENTS.md` checkpoint contract):** **confirmed — mandatory.** The hook grants no authority and blocks nothing, but it installs shared runtime machinery into every ref transaction of the common `.git`, covering all worktrees at once. Both of its failure modes — interfering with a legitimate transaction, and silently not firing while agents come to rely on it — are invisible from the implementer's own vantage, which is exactly the over-trust `DEC-0044-009` warns about. A non-implementer must look at it before it becomes ambient infrastructure.

- [x] **0044-16** PREREQ: 0044-16:0044-14, 0044-16:0044-15 Stop the mandatory hygiene check from blocking unrelated agents on a commit that is merely in flight. Claim: `TODO-Harry-Kira-0044-16-20260822T184500Z.md`; owner_token: `agent:harry-kira-20260822t184500z:0044-16:20260822T184500Z`. REF: `42e80f6e7412616999f42a865e3eefe8c985c85a`; candidate tip `e3561d47b4e2e0ddc4ebbf2a5af1bd8f813ab13a`; previous implementation REF `59e5fd79d1501f21ff08d7f6a305f7f97d37ee0b`. Prior review `a141a493817f57ecf076180ccd2854f20207d0a4` remains append-only `rejected`; corrective independent re-review `4b6db41f5e66958eb62b7d0798f4ff7f952c9e4d` is `accepted`.
  - **Context (observed live on 2026-08-22, twice within 35 minutes):** `_src/tools/check_integration_hygiene.py` samples **every** registered worktree — 108 at the time — and raises `FOREIGN_STAGED_TREE` whenever one's index differs from its `HEAD`. Under `AGENTS.md` a non-zero exit is a **stop, not a warning**, so any single worktree in that state halts every other agent's pre-mutation gate.
    - **14:47Z:** `Harry-Neelix-20260822T164100Z` could not start `0043-05` because `.worktrees/0038-30` had a divergent index. Its owner (`Kathryn-Icheb-20260822T144500Z`, unrelated Task) was simply mid-commit; it finished at 14:52Z and the same check then returned `PASS`/exit `0` at unchanged 108 worktrees.
    - **15:16Z–15:21Z:** `.worktrees/0038-32` held 8 staged paths during an ordinary editing phase and simultaneously blocked **three** lines: `0044-02` preparation (no branch, claim or proposal could be created), `0044-03` preparation, and the commit of `0043-05` — whose implementer had already regenerated and validated the work (5 pages, `--check` 0 deviations, 16 focused tests green) and could not land it.
    - In all four cases the blocked agent acted **correctly**: it did not touch the foreign worktree and escalated instead of deciding. Nothing was wrong, and the stops were still right under the current rule. That is the defect.
  - **Why it gets worse, not better:** the chance that *at least one* of N active agents is inside the seconds between `git add` and `git commit` grows with N; with an editing phase that holds an index for minutes it approaches certainty. A single instantaneous sample cannot distinguish a commit in flight from an abandoned staged tree — they are byte-identical in that sample. The only countermeasure available today is asking every agent to commit more often, which is coordination overhead standing in for a missing signal.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** The check can distinguish transient from stale. At minimum it re-samples a worktree that trips `FOREIGN_STAGED_TREE` after a bounded delay and reports it only if it is **still** divergent, and it reports the offending index's age so a reader can tell "0.4s old" from "11 hours old" without rerunning anything. A genuinely abandoned foreign staged tree still trips the check — proven by a hermetic fixture, not asserted. `MAIN_WORKTREE_DIRTY` and the stale-after-ref-move signature are **not** touched: neither is transient by nature, and weakening them would re-open exactly what `DEC-0044-010` exists to prevent. Exit `2` remains a failure, never a pass.
  - **Explicit scope boundary — read before mutating:** whether an unrelated worktree's staged tree should **block** or merely **advise** is a change to a gate that governs the start, validation and integration of other work units. It therefore meets the canonical `cross-item-blast-radius` predicate and requires a conforming `decision-record@v1` plus an independent Architect scope review **before** that mutation. Adding a re-sample and an age field, without changing what blocks, does **not** meet the predicate and needs no such record — start there if you want progress before the decision lands. Do not quietly convert the finding to advisory as a side effect of "fixing the false positive".
  - **Definition of Done:** Committed with tests for transient-not-reported, stale-still-reported, and the age field; real numbers reported; `docs/pipeline/tools.md` and wherever the check is described state the new behaviour and what remains uncovered; the hard root preflight from `DEC-0044-015` stays required and is restated, not relaxed. If the investigation shows the re-sample cannot be made reliable, that is reported rather than a weaker check shipped.
  - **Implementation completion (2026-08-22, `agent:harry-kira-20260822t184500z:0044-16:20260822T184500Z`):** `FOREIGN_STAGED_TREE` candidates are re-sampled once after a shared bounded 2.0-second delay and reported only when their index remains divergent; every persistent finding retains the same blocking code/exit behavior and adds `index_age_seconds`, `index_mtime_utc`, and `resample_delay_seconds`. Hermetic validation: 6/6 tests pass, including transient commit completion (no finding), persistent staged tree (finding remains), deterministic 39,600.000-second/11-hour age with parseable UTC mtime, unchanged `MAIN_WORKTREE_DIRTY`, and unchanged stale-after-ref-move signature. The real default-delay fixture completed in 2.746 s (wall 2.87 s); a full rerun after fail-closed index-stat hardening passed in 13.331 s. Live read-only scan with this ordinary dirty item worktree: PASS across 115 worktrees in 50.61 s. Focused automation safety PASS/0; process-doc reports byte-identical to `main` (29 findings); absent-repository probe returned exit 2. The hard root preflight and all-worktree blocking scope remain unchanged. No acceptance, checkpoint crossing, integration, Feature closure, `DONE.md` move, or `main` advance was performed.

Activation is fail-closed per runtime: before creating a new round, prove the coordinator and candidates expose the v1.11.0 tools; if not, refresh/restart the affected runtime and report the unavailable mechanism rather than silently emulating an atomic award with `send`.

  - **Definition of Done:** The operative autodocs documents agree on the state machine and chain rules; no prose still instructs a coordinator to use manual first-accept-wins `AWARD`/`WITHDRAWN` exchanges for new work; role/capability authority is unchanged; the external baseline and runtime-reload evidence are pinned; the full relevant process/tool tests pass; and an end-to-end two-chain pilot records exact offer IDs, winners, hidden-tier evidence and absence of duplicate starts without placing an unawarded peer on standby.

A defect can duplicate execution, hide eligible capacity, or violate prerequisite/checkpoint ordering across teams, so an independent privileged Integrator must verify both the governance projection and the executable race/failure-path evidence before activation.

## Feature: 0041 — Worker Isolation by Clone/Push and Simplified Check-in Semantics

**Authority:** Three explicit customer decisions of 2026-08-18, recorded verbatim as `RQ-SRC-02` in [`docs/dossiers/re-intake-worker-isolation-and-checkin.md`](docs/dossiers/re-intake-worker-isolation-and-checkin.md).

**Goal:** Get the development pipeline moving again. Worker checkouts become real clones that publish by push, the fragile two-commit closure is replaced by a single self-describing check-in, and `REF` tagging moves downstream into acceptance.

**Trigger of record:** `/tmp/autodocs/.git` was a **symlink** onto the canonical `.git`, so the worker checkout shared object store, refs, `HEAD` **and index** with `~/devel/autodocs`. A commit in one moved the other's `HEAD` while its working tree stayed put. That alone produced the appearance that `0038-18` had never been started, and it briefly produced a false accusation of misreporting against the implementing session — withdrawn in finding G of the requirements baseline. The implementer had worked correctly throughout.

**Capability constraint (finding I):** Sandboxed agents may not execute Git (`SANDBOX.md`), and none of the three workflow tools contains any branch, merge, clone or push function — zero matches across 9527 lines of `runner-host/perplexity-cpu-loop.js`, `runner-host/run-loop.sh` and `_src/tools/provision_tmp_worktree.sh`. Creation of the branch and the checkout therefore falls to the privileged host side. This is the only option that does not violate the capability classes, not a matter of preference.

**Note:** The branch naming convention is **already settled** — bare item IDs, Task branched off its Feature branch ([`docs/pipeline/branch-workflow.md`](docs/pipeline/branch-workflow.md)). What is missing is ownership and implementation, not the convention.

**Feature Definition of Done:** A worker receives a pre-provisioned clone on the branch of its assigned item, works there without any effect on the canonical working tree, and publishes by push; a single substantive check-in carries ticket ID and base-ref in a machine-readable form; the separate bookkeeping commit is gone from the authority documents; `REF` is required at acceptance rather than at `[x]`/`[w]`; and `TODO.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `SANDBOX.md`, `branch-workflow.md` and `task-acceptance.md` agree.

- [x] **0041-01** Replace the shared-checkout provisioner with a clone-based one that the privileged host runs per assigned item. REF: `8aafc0cb4`. Claim: `TODO-seven-naomi-0041-01-20260821T000000Z.md`; owner_token: `agent:seven-naomi:0041-01:20260821T000000Z`.
  - **Closure (2026-08-21):** Substantive delivery (`_src/tools/provision_worker_clone.sh`, `docs/pipeline/worker-clone-provisioning.md`) was already committed at `8aafc0cb4` but never bookkept. Independently re-verified all acceptance criteria and DoD points against a disposable throwaway repo under `/tmp` (never against the canonical repo or a tracked worktree): correct branch/parent derivation with `main` fallback and explicit notice, isolated clone with its own object store/refs/HEAD/index, canonical `git status`/`HEAD` byte-identical before/after, idempotent rerun, `.git`-symlink refusal, and registered-worktree refusal — all passed. `provision_tmp_worktree.sh` was confirmed rescoped (not left as a silent second mechanism) by the independent `0038-22` repair to a disjoint self-service scenario, with an explicit disclaiming header naming this script. `docs/pipeline/worker-clone-provisioning.md` documents who runs it and when. No test suite exists for the script, but the Task's Definition of Done does not require one (unlike `0041-06`'s), so this is not a gap. No fix was required; see claim for full evidence.
  - **Requirements covered:** `RQ-WT-01` … `RQ-WT-05`.
  - **Context:** `_src/tools/provision_tmp_worktree.sh` currently calls `git worktree add` on a hard-wired `tmp-work` branch, and its header advertises as a benefit that commits are "instantly durable" in the shared object store. That coupling is precisely the cause of the trigger (finding J). The clone deliberately gives it up: durability now begins at push.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** The provisioner takes the item ID as input, creates the branch from its correct parent per `branch-workflow.md` when absent, and provisions the worker checkout by `git clone` with its own object store, refs, `HEAD` and index. Running it leaves `git status` in the canonical tree unchanged — proven by a before/after comparison retained as evidence. It refuses to run when the target path is a `.git` symlink or a registered worktree of the canonical repository, and says which it found. It stays idempotent and must not discard uncommitted worker edits that survived a `/tmp` reap.
  - **Definition of Done:** Committed; the old shared-checkout path is removed or explicitly refused rather than left as a silent second mechanism; the header comment no longer advertises shared-object-store durability; `docs/pipeline/` documents who runs it and when.
  - **Integration review:** not mandatory. **No-checkpoint justification (architect):** the change is confined to provisioning of a disposable checkout and is objectively checkable (canonical tree unaffected); it is re-examined at `0041-05`.

- [x] **0041-02** PREREQ: 0041-02:0041-01 Establish the self-describing check-in and remove the separate bookkeeping commit from the authority documents.
  - **Hold — do not integrate (2026-08-25/26, Discovery Project Lead `michael`; visibility record, not a new checkpoint and not Task ownership):** Re-pinned onto `main` `d401aeb069371934ed349f5b59b9cae5051dbfbc` after the Culber preservation commit landed; the earlier hold candidate `d84a783e266936b3a0d6ae962836da81c7fd3c87` was based on `8a364e000` and is not an ancestor of this tip. Branch `0041-02` tip `8b1afb933f0f9029d09c2fd3e9660aad3a8fa9a3` is `[x]` on that branch only. Authoritative `main` still has this Task `[ ]`. Independent Architect verdict `scope-not-ready-for-mutation` is commit `1bc504e4bafbc21d23474cfdc3b6ec2eede1d23c` on `review-0041-02-scope-data-20260823T160421Z`, report `docs/dossiers/0041-02-gate-scope-review.md`. That review is not an ancestor of this `main` tip or of `8b1afb933f`. `0041-03`/`0041-04`/`0041-06` currently sit on historical `47fb026016`, not on `8b1afb933f` and not on this `main`; do not treat them as start-ready. Do not merge `8b1afb933f`. Lift only when a conforming `decision-record@v1` exists on current `main` and the Architect stop is superseded or explicitly waived by recorded Management authority. Does not overwrite claim `TODO-Gabriel-Keyla-0041-02-20260825T000800Z.md`. Does not change `Integration review: mandatory`.
  - **Requirements covered:** `RQ-CI-01` … `RQ-CI-05`.
  - **Context (finding H):** The two-commit closure is structurally fragile — the second commit depends on the first one's hash, so it can only be written afterwards, and nothing forces it. Four Tasks sit in exactly that state today: `0007-01`, `0037-37`, `0038-02` and `0038-18`. This removes the cause rather than monitoring it.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** A defined, machine-readable trailer in the substantive commit message carries the ticket ID and the base-ref the change was made against; the format is specified once and used everywhere. `AGENTS.md` ("Completing implementation work"), the `TODO.md` header and `branch-workflow.md` no longer require a separate bookkeeping commit and no longer describe the hash-injection dance. Where a document previously required the two-step, it states the replacement instead of merely deleting the requirement.
  - **Definition of Done:** Committed; no authority document still demands a separate bookkeeping commit for `[p]` → `[x]`/`[w]`; the trailer format is documented with at least one worked example; existing terminal Tasks are not retroactively invalidated.
  - **Integration review:** **mandatory.** **Rationale (architect):** this changes the binding closure procedure that every agent follows. An error here either leaves two contradictory closure procedures in force or silently drops the traceability the trailer is meant to carry.

- [x] **0041-03** PREREQ: 0041-03:0041-02 Move `REF` tagging from implementation completion to the acceptance transition.
  - **Requirements covered:** `RQ-REF-01` … `RQ-REF-03`.
  - **Context (finding K):** The `TODO.md` header currently defines `[x]` as requiring a "real substantive `REF`", and `AGENTS.md` and `task-acceptance.md` repeat it. Changing one and not the others reproduces `T8` — documentation and binding instruction disagreeing, with the instruction winning.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** `[x]`/`[w]` no longer require a `REF`. The `[x]` → `✓` transition owns it, and the documents state explicitly in which cases it stays mandatory and in which it is optional, rather than leaving "often optional" to interpretation. `TODO.md` header, `AGENTS.md` and `task-acceptance.md` agree word for word on the new gate.
  - **Definition of Done:** Committed; a grep for the old requirement across the authority documents returns nothing that still binds; existing `REF` records stay valid and are not rewritten.
  - **Integration review:** not mandatory. **No-checkpoint justification (architect):** the change relaxes a gate rather than granting capability, and its consistency is mechanically checkable by the grep named in the Definition of Done. Re-examined at `0041-05`.

- [x] **0041-04** PREREQ: 0041-04:0041-01, 0041-04:0041-02 Give the runner tooling a push path with an item-scoped guard.
  - **Requirements covered:** `RQ-WT-03`, `RQ-WT-06`; resolves the open point of section 5 of the requirements baseline.
  - **Context:** Publication by push is the only way work leaves the clone, and no tool implements it today. The guard needs the assigned item ID; whether `perplexity-cpu-loop.js` already carries that value is to be determined during implementation, and if it does not, it becomes a new provisioner input.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** The host side can push the worker branch to the canonical repository. A push whose target branch does not match the assigned item ID is refused with a clear message and a non-zero exit. The refusal is covered by a test or a documented reproducible check. No force-push to a protected ref is possible through this path.
  - **Definition of Done:** Committed; the guard demonstrably refuses a mismatched target; where the item ID had to become a new input, the change is documented at the provisioner and its caller.
  - **Integration review:** not mandatory. **No-checkpoint justification (architect):** the Task adds a narrowly scoped publication path with a refusing guard and no acceptance authority; its failure mode is a refused push, which is loud rather than silent. Re-examined at `0041-05`.

Reject mismatched Task IDs, non-ancestor/missing Base-Refs, nonterminal committed markers, stale refs, partial trees, and any manifest that requests the removed implementation-bookkeeping phase. Preserve journal, rollback, compare-and-swap, path isolation, provenance, and post-publication recovery guarantees from `0038-02`. ## Feature: 0039 — Process improvement

**Reservation — reserved for privileged agents, tbd.:** No agent may autonomously claim or start this Feature or any of its Tasks. A current user must explicitly select the Task and designate the owning session as privileged before its marker may leave `[u]`. The existence of this Feature, a terminal prerequisite, or an available terminal tool is not assignment or authorization.

**Goal:** Turn the two independent studies under `docs/dossiers/` into controlled, piloted, measurable standard processes: one for defining and decomposing Features, and one for creating, qualifying, reusing, improving, and retiring automation tools. Preserve the distinction between product/work definition and tool/process improvement; neither process may silently authorize architecture, approval, safety acceptance, external mutation, or arbitrary code execution.

**Study baseline:** `docs/dossiers/feature-definition-process-study.docx` and `docs/dossiers/tool-creation-improvement-process-study.docx` are informative analysis inputs, not approved process definitions or Automotive SPICE capability claims. Later Tasks must disposition their recommendations against current authority, Feature `0037` issue-store/cutover contracts, Feature `0038` chore automation, and retained evidence rather than adopting prose wholesale.

**Suggestion reconciliation baseline:** The fail-closed runner-transaction and approval-readiness suggestions already have authoritative coverage in Feature `0038` and must not be duplicated here. The retained Feature `0036` page-i18n completeness prototype lacks an authoritative implementation Task and is represented by reserved pilot Task `0039-03`; its historical suggestion remains provenance, not a second backlog authority.

**Feature Definition of Done:** Both standard processes are approved, versioned, documented, tooled where appropriate, piloted on representative work, integrated with the authoritative issue/task lifecycle and runner action model, and measured without unsupported capability claims. Roles, tailoring, configuration/change control, independent assurance, failure/recovery, adoption, exception, supersession, and retirement are explicit. The page-i18n pilot is dispositioned, every imported suggestion has one authoritative owner or explicit rejection/supersession, and a privileged independent review confirms that the processes improve repeatability without creating self-authorization or an uncontrolled script ecosystem.

### Campaign A — Standard process definition

- [x] **0039-04** Define and introduce the privileged Task-acceptance and Feature-closure convention, with a DOCX/PDF dossier and adapted process/agent instructions.
  - **Claim (2026-08-17):** The current user selected this work and retained this session's explicit privileged designation. Claimed via `TODO-zed-0039-04-20260817-131714-a3facd2d095e.md`, owner_token: agent:zed:0039-04:20260817-131714-a3facd2d095e, base_commit `7df56ab6686b2b5bc45efc1d99455e4e838530ab`.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** Define canonical accepted state `accepted` rendered as `✓`; distinguish implementation/disposition completion from acceptance; prohibit sandboxed/grunt acceptance promotion; require an independently assigned privileged reviewer to inspect the exact Task contract, work products, validation/evidence, material findings, and transitive non-accepted prerequisite closure; define bottom-up acceptance, accepted dispositions, rejection/inconclusive outcomes, invalidation, high-risk acceptance-before-start gates, Feature aggregate review, authority boundaries, migration/cutover, records, metrics, and machine-enforcement needs. Produce a DOCX/PDF dossier covering affected documents/processes, daily operation, benefits/risks, and careful Automotive SPICE relationships; adapt current agentic and pipeline instructions without appropriating foreign tool claims.

## Feature: 0038 — Transactional Task Work, Reliable Evidence, and Chore Automation

**Goal:** Make routine Task work fail closed, reproducible, quick to resume, and cheap to review. Replace copied shell/Python orchestration with narrow tested tools; prevent validation, bookkeeping, claim, index, generated-output, evidence, and cleanup mistakes; and carry reusable semantics into Feature `0037` without creating a competing issue store or runner protocol.

**Evidence baseline (2026-08-16):** The Task `0036-05` singleton request changed `TODO.md` and wildcard-deleted claims before project validation; historical closure commits `9e033f32`, `9c4795bb`, and `cd6d8db1` show heredoc/Task-boundary repair churn; `723b485d` and `a1cbbbdc` repaired stale pre-amend REFs; commit `50b20829` retained 184 evidence files/10,384 lines including duplicate source/scripts; Feature `0021` had green focused/generation checks but was later archived not accepted; current build artifacts show repeated npm/cache/watchdog/browser retries, mutable `run-current` evidence, fixed-path exports, empty/partial attempts, and exit-zero reports containing failed required cases. Current legacy claims also expose marker/claim drift, derived-output scope overlap, context overflow, and inaccurate publication state. These are retained lessons, not claims that every historical run was invalid.

**Compatibility boundary:** This Feature hardens the current legacy workflow. It must reuse the approved Feature `0037` contracts where available, must not weaken or bypass `0037-07`, and must map every surviving primitive to `0037-46.01` or retire it at `0037-46.02`. Tasks that remain useful after cutover become typed queue/issue-store actions; legacy-only wrappers do not become a second permanent protocol.

**Dependency repair (2026-08-16):** The initial graph omitted several direct producer/consumer gates and made the `0038-16` handoff semantically impossible: it required mapping primitives into `0037-46.01` but could not start until aggregate `0037-46` had already closed. The smallest intent-preserving repair orders recovery, results, environment identity, evidence, retention, generation, and mutator classification by their consumed contracts; binds legacy doctors/results/readiness to the terminal `0037` bootstrap, runner-result, and review-package contracts; splits handoff preparation from post-activation rollout; and makes `0037-07`, `0037-46.01`, and `0037-21` consume the corresponding readiness, handoff, and retirement deliverables. Historical incident owners remain fixtures, not artificial prerequisites.

**Feature Definition of Done:** Historical incident fixtures fail at the intended gate; a sandboxed agent can discover, claim, generate, validate, commit, close, retain evidence, recover, and resume a representative Task without handwritten destructive orchestration or direct execution; unrelated staged/unstaged/untracked work and foreign claims survive; required failures cannot produce a passing aggregate result; compact evidence remains traceable without copied source dumps; stale/partial artifacts have an explicit lifecycle; and queue activation has an executed handoff/retirement map with no duplicate authority.

- [x] **0038-29** PREREQ: 0038-29:0038-26 Provide a bounded publication mechanism that releases exactly one approved subtree, digest-bound, without dragging the rest of the site with it. Claims: `TODO-Kathryn-Tuvok-0038-29-20260822T113500Z.md`, `TODO-Kathryn-Chakotay-0038-29-20260822T124000Z.md` (review-finding rework) (dispatcht von Projektleiter `kathryn`; unprivilegiert, kein Checkpoint, keine main-Bewegung). REF: `fcc6b63cd`, rework REF: `25df30367`
  - **Context (real incident, 2026-08-22):** Feature `0019` had a management-approved export subtree, pinned by tree digest `7c514686ba7241416dbab340b4cad9abe032e2c6150e807b302efac363d08283` (2,248 files), and an independent narrow exclusion check with verdict `sauber`. The publication still could not be executed as approved. `_src/publish.sh` synchronises a **fixed** set of directories (`PUBLIC_DIRS`: `ar classes en es flags fr hi ko modules namespaces pt ru services zh`) and files (`PUBLIC_FILES`: `index.html style.css fold.js review.js`) from the local generated tree. The approved subtree is **not in that list at all**, so the script would not have published the approved artifact — while synchronising **4,133 unrelated, unreviewed paths** against deploy baseline `70fcf5935b5725c92ceccd234a8087268eaee28f`. The privileged publication operator `worf-krell-20260822t102000z` detected this after a green preflight and stopped before any write. Management resolved the single case by broadening the authorisation to the full site sync and explicitly accepting the risk (`docs/campaign-evidence/eclipse-score-v0.6.0-closure-candidate/release-authorization-20260822.md`, commit `388018fdd`). That record states in terms that the broadening is a **case decision, not a fix**, and that every future subtree release faces the same forced choice until this Task closes.
  - **Problem statement:** The repository can publish *everything* or *nothing*. It cannot publish *what was approved*. Approval is issued per artifact with a digest; publication ignores both. That mismatch means an approval can neither be honoured nor mechanically enforced at the moment it matters most — the one action that leaves the machine and cannot be taken back.
  - **Requirements covered:** extends `0038-26`'s parameterisation of publication identity/destination to publication **scope**.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** A tested tool publishes exactly one caller-named subtree to the configured destination. It takes the expected tree digest as a required argument, recomputes it over the actual source directory immediately before writing, and **refuses to publish on mismatch** — the digest procedure is the one implemented in `_src/tools/prepare_score_curation_export.py` (SHA-256 over path-sorted records of UTF-8 relative POSIX path bytes, one NUL byte, and the raw 32-byte per-file SHA-256), and it is documented where the tool is registered so a reviewer can reproduce it by hand. Paths outside the named subtree are never created, modified or deleted; this is asserted by a test that starts from a destination containing unrelated content and proves it survives byte-identically. Deletion inside the destination subtree happens only for paths the source no longer contains, and any deletion is reported before it is performed. The existing safety guards of `_src/publish.sh` remain in force and are not weakened: no private path (`_src`, `output`, `.gitignore`) may enter the destination, and identity/destination stay explicit parameters with no embedded defaults (`0038-26`). A dry-run mode reports the complete intended effect — created, modified, deleted, with counts and a bounded sample — and is the mode used to satisfy any pre-publication disclosure obligation. The tool never invents authority: it requires an explicit authorisation reference (a commit or record ID) and writes it into the publication evidence.
  - **Definition of Done:** Committed with focused tests covering at minimum: digest match, digest **mismatch** (must refuse), unrelated destination content preserved, in-subtree deletion reported, private-path guard, and dry-run output completeness. Registered in [`docs/pipeline/tools.md`](docs/pipeline/tools.md) with the digest procedure written out. `_src/publish.sh` remains the whole-site publisher and is **not** replaced; the relationship between the two is documented so nobody has to guess which one applies. `python3 _src/tools/automation_safety.py` passes for the new tool. No credentials, remote configuration or destination defaults are embedded.
  - **Explicit non-goals:** This Task does **not** publish anything, does not alter the Feature `0019` release already authorised, and does not change who may authorise a publication. It supplies the mechanism; authority remains with management per `DEC-0019-001` and its successors.
  - **Completion evidence (2026-08-22, `agent:kathryn-tuvok:0038-29:20260822T113500Z`, unprivileged Implementer, dispatched by Projektleiter `kathryn`):** Substantive commit `fcc6b63cd` on branch `0038-29` (worktree `.worktrees/0038-29`, based off `main` at `2f3e29b2a`; pre-mutation `check_integration_hygiene.py --repo .worktrees/0038-29` → PASS, 102 registered worktrees). New `_src/tools/publish_approved_subtree.py` publishes exactly one caller-named subtree into a caller-named destination directory: `--expected-tree-digest` is required and recomputed over the actual source both at planning time and again **immediately before the first write** (mismatch → refusal, nothing written; the source inventory is additionally compared against the plan, so a source that changes between planning and writing is refused too); paths outside `<destination-root>/<subtree>` are never created, modified or deleted; deletions occur only for paths the source no longer contains, only inside the destination subtree, with the **complete** list printed before the first deletion; `--dry-run` is the disclosure mode and reports counts per category plus a bounded `--sample` with the remainder explicitly disclosed, writing nothing; the `_src/publish.sh` guards are retained and widened — no private component (`_src`, `output`, `.gitignore`, `.git`) may enter the destination as subtree **or** as a source path, and symlinks/non-regular source files, subtree escape (absolute, `..`, symlinked destination path) and source/destination overlap are refused; `--authorization-ref` is required and written into the JSON publication evidence, existing evidence is never overwritten, and evidence/journal may not be written inside the published subtree; every mutating step appends a durable outcome/recovery record to the optional `--journal`, and after writing the destination subtree digest is recomputed and must equal the approved digest. **No embedded defaults:** destination and subtree are required arguments; the tool performs no VCS or network operation, creates no commit and pushes nothing, so a commit identity does not occur in it at all — the `PUBLISH_IDENTITY_*`/`PUBLISH_REMOTE` requirement from `0038-26` continues to govern the downstream, separately authorised operator commit/push step, and this reading is documented in `docs/pipeline/tools.md` and reported to the dispatcher rather than resolved silently. **Digest procedure finding:** the named reference implementation `_src/tools/prepare_score_curation_export.py` exists only on branches `0019`/`0019-10` (introduced `04a89097b`, merged `58b35f1e5`) and **not on `main`**, so it could not be imported as code; Being `advisory`, it affects neither the verdict nor `policy_errors`; substantively it describes exactly the bounded, pre-announced in-subtree deletion this Task requires. The implementer produced exactly those, each independently red. That is not a coincidence worth ignoring.
  - **Diagnosis:** implementer tests are **confirmatory** — they demonstrate that the fix works. Reviewer tests are **refutational** — they look for what the fix broke. All four defects were *neighbours* of the change: one field over, one count over, one role over. Nothing here is about care or competence; the two activities ask different questions, and only one of them was required.
  - **Acceptance criteria:** (1) For any change touching counting, identity, serialisation or gate behaviour, the completion evidence shows the failing case **red before the fix** and green after, with the real command and output — not a claim. (2) The completion evidence names **at least two adjacent cases** the implementer deliberately checked, and their result; naming a case that turned out fine is a pass, naming none is not. (3) Where the change asserts an **invariant over a set** (deduplication, merging, closure, ordering), a property-based test is required and its case count is reported. (4) The three requirements are stated where implementers actually read them — the `TODO.md` header contract and `AGENTS.md`'s completion section — not only in a dossier.
  - **Deliberately bounded:** this does **not** apply to every Task. It applies to the four named change kinds. A rule that demands adversarial evidence everywhere will be satisfied ritually and stop meaning anything — which is the failure mode the checkpoint attribute already had to be protected from.
